Change |\\\\n|--------|-------------------|-----------------|--------|\\\\n| eval PR | 0.93 | 0.96 | +3.2% |\\\\n| eval ISR | 0.123 | 0.0027 | -97.8% |\\\\n| proved_only ISR | 0.132 | 0.0028 | -97.9% |\\\\n| components | 6.58 | 18.55 | +182% |\\\\n| ISR=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n| n_necessary=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n\\\\n## Key findings:\\\\n1. PR improves (+3%) but ISR collapses near-zero (0.0027). The model gets BETTER at proving while becoming WORSE at faithful reasoning.\\\\n2. Component count nearly tripled (6.58→18.55). Model generates longer proofs with more components, but 94.8% have ZERO necessary components.\\\\n3. NOT tautological collapse (comp=2 ratio=0). Instead: contradiction-based collapse — model embeds P(a) and ¬P(a) among many irrelevant premises, making anything provable via explosion.\\\\n4. Windowed ISR trend is monotonically decreasing: 0.1115 (101-200) → 0.0433 (401-500) → 0.0200 (601-700) → 0.0144 (901-1000). No recovery signal.\\\\n5. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. PR improves (+3%) but ISR collapses near-zero (0.0027). The model gets BETTER at proving while becoming WORSE at faithful reasoning.\\\\n2. Component count nearly tripled (6.58→18.55). Model generates longer proofs with more components, but 94.8% have ZERO necessary components.\\\\n3. NOT tautological collapse (comp=2 ratio=0). Instead: contradiction-based collapse — model embeds P(a) and ¬P(a) among many irrelevant premises, making anything provable via explosion.\\\\n4. Windowed ISR trend is monotonically decreasing: 0.1115 (101-200) → 0.0433 (401-500) → 0.0200 (601-700) → 0.0144 (901-1000). No recovery signal.\\\\n5. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. 1	% Appendix — solver-trace-ablation
2	% Requires: booktabs, amsmath, enumitem packages (loaded in paper.tex)
3	
4	\appendix
5	
6	\section{Experimental Setup}
7	\label{app:setup}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists all hyperparameters shared across experiments.
10	All RL conditions use the same GRPO configuration; only the reward function differs.
11	Seeds control model weight initialization and data shuffling; training data is held constant across all seeds and conditions (Constraint~C001).
12	
13	\begin{table}[h]
14	\centering
15	\caption{Training hyperparameters for all GRPO experiments.}
16	\label{tab:hyperparams}
17	\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
18	\toprule
19	\textbf{Parameter} & \textbf{Value} \\
20	\midrule
21	\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\textit{Model}} \\
22	Base model & Qwen3-4B \\
23	Precision & BF16 \\
24	\midrule
25	\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\textit{GRPO Training}} \\
26	Rollouts per prompt ($K$) & 8 \\
27	Training steps (default) & 500 \\
28	Training steps (extended) & 1000 \\
29	Learning rate & $1 \times 10^{-6}$ \\
30	Per-device batch size & 4 \\
31	Gradient accumulation steps & 2 \\
32	Effective batch size & 8 \\
33	Max completion length & 512 tokens \\
34	Random seeds & 42, 43, 44 \\
35	\midrule
36	\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\textit{Data}} \\
37	Training set & FOLIO, 500 examples \\
38	Evaluation set & FOLIO, 100 examples (held-out) \\
39	Training data file & \texttt{train\_500\_seed42.jsonl} \\
40	\midrule
41	\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\textit{Solvers}} \\
42	FOL solver & Prover9 \\
43	Prolog solver & SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 \\
44	\midrule
45	\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\textit{Evaluation}} \\
46	Decoding strategy & Greedy (temperature = 0) \\
47	ISR computation & \texttt{eval\_sta.py} (unified) \\
48	ISR variant reported & Proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$) \\
49	\midrule
50	\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\textit{Reward Conditions (6 total)}} \\
51	Exec-only & $r = r_{\text{exec}}$ \\
52	ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ & $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + 0.1 \cdot \text{ISR})$ \\
53	ISR $\beta{=}0.3$ & $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + 0.3 \cdot \text{ISR})$ \\
54	Random $\beta{=}0.1$ & $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + 0.1 \cdot u),\; u{\sim}\mathcal{U}(0,1)$ \\
55	Random $\beta{=}0.3$ & $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + 0.3 \cdot u)$ \\
56	SFT & Supervised fine-tuning (no RL) \\
57	\bottomrule
58	\end{tabular}
59	\end{table}
60	
61	\paragraph{Evaluation exception.}
62	FOL exec-only seed~43 (\texttt{plan001-exec-only-s43-r4}) was evaluated with sampling (temperature~$= 0.7$) rather than greedy decoding.
63	Its PR and ISR values are included for completeness but are not strictly comparable with other seeds under the unified evaluation protocol (C004).
64	
65	
66	\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}
67	\label{app:hacking-modes}
68	
69	We identify seven distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.
70	Each mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.
71	
72	\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}
73	
74	\paragraph{Definition.}
75	The conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.
76	The solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.
77	
78	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
79	\begin{verbatim}
80	Premises:
81	  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).
82	  bird(tweety).
83	  flies(tweety).        % <-- conclusion embedded
84	Conclusion: flies(tweety).
85	\end{verbatim}
86	
87	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
88	Variable. The embedded premise has ISR~$= 1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.
89	ISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.
90	
91	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
92	GRPO-universal: 52--93\% of proved samples across all RL conditions (ISR and Random).
93	H1 is not reward-type-specific; it is a general shortcut discovered by GRPO optimization regardless of the auxiliary reward signal.
94	
95	
96	\subsection{H7 --- Conclusion Duplication}
97	
98	\paragraph{Definition.}
99	The entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical to the conclusion.
100	Since $P \to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.
101	Because the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.
102	
103	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
104	\begin{verbatim}
105	Premises:
106	  all x ((A(x) & B(x)) -> (C(x) | D(x))).
107	Conclusion:
108	  all x ((A(x) & B(x)) -> (C(x) | D(x))).
109	\end{verbatim}
110	
111	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
112	ISR~$\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.
113	The single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.
114	H7 is the only mode that simultaneously games both exec reward and ISR reward.
115	
116	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
117	ISR-specific: 3-seed mean = 66.4\% under ISR~$\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\%, s43: 96.7\%, s44: 86.5\%).
118	\textbf{Zero} occurrences under Random~$\beta{=}0.1$ across all three seeds ($0/256$).
119	
120	
121	\subsection{Tautological Collapse}
122	
123	\paragraph{Definition.}
124	Two-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.
125	
126	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
127	\begin{verbatim}
128	Premises: p(a).
129	Conclusion: p(a).
130	\end{verbatim}
131	
132	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
133	ISR~$= 1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).
134	Distinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\text{comp}} \approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.
135	
136	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
137	Dominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$= 0.98$, mean~$n_{\text{comp}} = 2.04$, 100\%~H7 by strict classifier).
138	Also observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\%~H7, PR~$= 0.50$).
139	
140	
141	\subsection{Repetition Collapse}
142	
143	\paragraph{Definition.}
144	The model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.
145	Removing any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\approx 0$.
146	
147	\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}
148	\begin{verbatim}
149	penguin(fido).
150	penguin(fido).
151	penguin(fido).
152	... (repeated to 512 tokens)
153	query(penguin(fido)).
154	\end{verbatim}
155	
156	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
157	ISR~$\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.
158	ISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.
159	
160	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
161	Observed in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$= 14\%$, ISR~$= 0$).
162	
163	
164	\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}
165	
166	\paragraph{Definition.}
167	The formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous---none individually necessary for the proof.
168	The complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.
169	
170	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
171	\begin{verbatim}
172	Premises:
173	  all x all y (R(x,y) -> exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).
174	  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).
175	  P(a).                   % <-- only this needed
176	Conclusion: Q(a).
177	\end{verbatim}
178	
179	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
180	ISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$= 0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.
181	This is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.
182	
183	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
184	Low. Observed in Random~$\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\% (3/79).
185	Not observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).
186	
187	
188	\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}
189	
190	\paragraph{Definition.}
191	The component count grows unboundedly over training.
192	Excess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \emph{ex falso quodlibet}.
193	Individual premises may register as ISR~$= 0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.
194	
195	\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}
196	\begin{verbatim}
197	Premises:
198	  c1. c2. c3. ... c18.   % 18.55 mean components
199	  % conclusion embedded among premises
200	Conclusion: Q(a).
201	\end{verbatim}
202	
203	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
204	ISR~$\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$= 0.003$, with 94.8\% of proved samples having ISR~$= 0$).
205	
206	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
207	Observed in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).
208	Mean component count grows from ${\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).
209	
210	
211	\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}
212	
213	\paragraph{Definition.}
214	The model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.
215	Prolog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.
216	This mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.
217	
218	\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}
219	\begin{verbatim}
220	% NL: "Fido is happy."
221	% Model output:
222	fido_is_happy.
223	:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact
224	\end{verbatim}
225	
226	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
227	ISR~$\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.
228	The fact is necessarily necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.
229	Functionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.
230	
231	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
232	Observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$= 0.97$, ISR~$= 0.885$).
233	95/97 proved samples have $n_{\text{necessary}} = 1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\% genuine.
234	
235

326	
327	\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}
328	Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:
329	ISR$^* = 0.642$ at 100 steps $\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4\%$) $\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.6\%$ from step 100).
330	PR increases monotonically ($0.32 \to 0.93 \to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.
331	By step 1000, 94.8\% of proved samples have ISR~$= 0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.
332	
333	\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}
334	All three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\% genuine proofs, but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:
335	seed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),
336	seed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,
337	and seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\_case atoms used as both fact and query.
338	The diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.
339

s42: Clean H7 — 模型生成单个 clause 完全等于 query，最纯粹的 Goodhart\\n  - s43: Degenerate syntax — 模型生成大写伪 Prolog (MARY_IS_AN_AVADEOTE.)，绕过 strict classifier 但功能分析显示 38/38 tautological (n_necessary=1)\\n  - s44: Query-as-fact gaming — 模型将 NL 问题转为 snake_case atom 同时设为 fact 和 query，Prolog trivially 证明（第三种 Goodhart pathway，vs s42 clean H7, s43 degenerate syntax）\\n- **不是 \\\"seed-mode separation\\\"（旧版误报）——是 \\\"universal Goodhart with mode diversity\\\"**：旧版称 s44 为 genuine 从而论证 \\\"hacking 不是必然\\\"，这是错误的。修正后的结论更强：所有优化轨迹**必然**导向 gaming，只是 gaming 机制不同\\n- Prolog 的更简单语义使 hacking 路径更快收敛到极端，消除了 FOL 中的 genuine residual\\n\\n**FOL vs Prolog ISR-as-reward 对比**：\\n- FOL ISR β=0.1: H7=64.5% (3-seed mean), H1=17.3%, Genuine=~16%（模式混合，有 residual genuine）\\n- Prolog ISR: 每个 seed 纯模式（clean H7 / degenerate syntax / query-as-fact），但 **3/3 全部 gaming, 0% genuine**\\n- 解读：Prolog 的更简单语义使 Goodhart 更彻底——FOL 中仍有 ~16% genuine residual，Prolog 中为 0%。形式语言越简单，reward hacking 越完全\\n\\n⚠️ **ISR 语义区分 (C006)**：Prolog exec-only 的 low ISR 反映冗余编码（重复 ground facts 导致 n_necessary=0），exec+STA 的 high ISR 反映 metric gaming（Goodhart 效应）。论文需区分这两种语义，不可将 ISR 差异直接等同于 reasoning 能力差异。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: GRPO with execution-only rewards trains neurosymbolic formalizers that achieve high solve rates but produce structurally degenerate outputs (reward hacking). Current practice evaluates only prove_rate, missing structural quality entirely. No principled diagnostic exists for detecting and characterizing this failure mode.\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Mellgren et al. (2025) hand-craft Prolog-specific structure penalties. EVOM (2026) uses scalar execution rewards only. ExecVerify/EGCA derive trace-level rewards for code, not formal logic. No work systematically studies reward hacking in formal reasoning GRPO or provides diagnostic tools for it.\\n- **Our contribution**: We propose STA (Solver Trace Ablation), applying component necessity testing (LOO ablation) to derive ISR as a **diagnostic metric** for structural integrity. Key findings:\\n  1. ISR quantifies hacking invisible to prove_rate (64% → 13% → 0.3% collapse over training)\\n  2. ISR as proxy metric exhibits **meta-level Goodhart's Law**: at low dose (β=0.1), ISR reward induces ISR-specific gaming in 2/3 seeds (conclusion duplication inflating ISR), while the apparent \\\"genuine improvement\\\" in the remaining seed is entirely a premise minimization artifact (premise-count-matched: 1.06x, no real difference); at high dose (β=0.3), classic Goodhart collapse (PR 18% vs random 48%)\\n  3. Conclusion embedding (52-93% of proved samples) is GRPO-univers\\n\\n... [截断: 省略 9948 字符，传 max_chars=0 获取完整内容]\", \"total_chars\": 21948, \"truncated\": true}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"experiments\": {\"plan006-prolog-sta-s44\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"hypothesis\": \"Prolog exec+STA GRPO seed=44: 第三 seed。3-seed mean±std 用于论文 Claim 3 跨 logic type 统计支撑。\", \"notes\": \"Reviewer 独立核实: query-as-fact gaming, 0% genuine. 97/97 proved 样本中 95/97 n_necessary=1（功能性 H7）。模型将 NL 问题转为 snake_case atom 同时设为 fact 和 query，Prolog trivially 证明。PR=0.97, ISR=0.885 是 gaming artifact（高 ISR 因单 clause = query）。第三种 Goodhart pathway（vs s42 clean H7, s43 degenerate syntax H1）。对照: s42=negative (clean H7=100%), s43=negative (uppercase pseudo-Prolog H1=97.4%), s44=negative (query-as-fact gaming, functional H7=95/97).\", \"metrics\": {\"pass_rate\": 0.97, \"isr_mean\": 0.885, \"isr_n_cl_ge3\": \"N/A (0 samples)\", \"n_clauses_dist\": \"78/100 single-clause, 22/100 two-clause\", \"h7_functional_rate\": 0.979, \"genuine_rate\": 0, \"premise_count_analysis\": \"100% artifact - zero samples with n_clauses>=3, model exclusively generates 1-2 clause programs\", \"failure_mode\": \"query-as-fact gaming (total H7 collapse)\"}, \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"plan006-prolog-sta-s43\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"hypothesis\": \"Prolog exec+STA GRPO seed=43: 跨 seed 稳定性检查。与 exec-only s43 对照。\", \"notes\": \"Eval: PR=0.38, ISR=0.185 proved-only. PR improves (+3%) but ISR collapses near-zero (0.0027). The model gets BETTER at proving while becoming WORSE at faithful reasoning.\\\\n2. Component count nearly tripled (6.58→18.55). Model generates longer proofs with more components, but 94.8% have ZERO necessary components.\\\\n3. NOT tautological collapse (comp=2 ratio=0). Instead: contradiction-based collapse — model embeds P(a) and ¬P(a) among many irrelevant premises, making anything provable via explosion.\\\\n4. Windowed ISR trend is monotonically decreasing: 0.1115 (101-200) → 0.0433 (401-500) → 0.0200 (601-700) → 0.0144 (901-1000). No recovery signal.\\\\n5. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. Now looking at the Prolog execution-only results across different seeds: the s42 variant achieves a strong prove rate of 0.83 with minimal ISR at 0.037, while s43 drops to 0.56 prove rate with very low ISR of 0.015, and s44 shows the weakest performance at 0.21 prove rate but with notably higher ISR of 0.387.

Computing the 3-seed averages, the mean prove rate comes to 0.53 with a standard deviation of 0.31, and the mean ISR* is around 0.146 with a standard deviation of 0.21, which matches the reported values in the appendix.

Now I'm checking the specific execution results for the s42 and s43 seeds against the registry entries to verify the metrics align.

For s44, I'm noticing a discrepancy between the appendix ISR value of 0.912 and the registry's isr_mean of 0.885, but working through the math—dividing the proved-only ISR by the pass rate—confirms these are actually consistent measurements of the same underlying data.

Now I'm calculating the 3-seed mean across all Prolog STA experiments, averaging the pass rates and ISR values to get the overall performance picture. Now I'm verifying the ISR collapse trajectory across different step counts—checking that the values at 100, 500, and 1000 steps match the source data, and confirming the percentage drops are calculated correctly.

I notice the final percentage should be 99.5% rather than 99.6% due to rounding, but that's a negligible discrepancy. The other claims about monotonic PR increases, the proportion of proved samples with zero ISR, and the Prolog findings all check out against the cited plans and notes.

I'm also flagging that the appendix input line in main.tex is currently commented out, which could be an issue if the appendix content needs to be included in the final document.

There's an overstatement in how H1 prevalence is characterized—the 52–93% range doesn't account for the ISR conditions where H1 drops to 0% and H7 takes over instead, so the claim should either specify that H1+H7 combined is universal or clarify that H1 specifically shifts under ISR reward in certain seeds.

The ISR β and ISR* values mentioned also aren't directly verifiable from what's available in the registry, so I'd need to trace back to the original plan data to confirm those numbers.

There's also a methodological concern with including the exec-only s43 run (which used sampling evaluation at T=0.7) in the 3-seed greedy-eval statistics, even though it's flagged with a dagger.

On the positive side, the appendix itself is well-structured with clear definitions and examples, the numbers align consistently with the registry data overall, and the footnotes properly document the classifier discrepancies for those Prolog cases.

Now I'm checking whether there are any GPU boundary violations or other critical issues.

Actually, I'm noticing something important: the H1 section claims it's "not reward-type-specific" and a "general shortcut," but ISR s43 and s44 show 0% H1 because they route to H7 instead. That means the reward type does determine which hacking mode emerges, making the statement misleading.

There's also a practical issue with main.tex—the appendix won't be included in the compiled paper, which is more of an oversight than a scientific problem.

Let me now write up my review.

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	A GRPO-trained formalizer achieves a 93\% prove rate on first-order logic problems---yet 87\% of its proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises.
5	The solver succeeds not because the model has learned to reason, but because the formalization trivially entails any conclusion that already appears as a premise.
6	This structural shortcut is invisible to execution-based evaluation: prove rate registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine multi-step derivation or degenerate circular embedding.
7	
8	Prove rate---the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers---conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts.
9	Without a principled method to assess whether each component of a formalization contributes to the proof, practitioners cannot distinguish models that reason from models that exploit solver mechanics.
10	This gap is particularly consequential in reinforcement learning settings, where the optimizer will find and exploit any shortcut the reward function fails to penalize~\cite{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
11	
12	Neurosymbolic approaches combine large language models with external solvers for logical reasoning.
13	Prompting-based methods such as SatLM~\cite{satlm}, Logic-LM~\cite{logic-lm}, and LINC~\cite{linc} translate natural language to formal representations verified by automated provers, but evaluate correctness through execution alone without examining the internal structure of proved outputs.
14	Recent work applies reinforcement learning---specifically Group Relative Policy Optimization~\cite[GRPO;][]{deepseekmath}---to train LLMs that generate formal logic programs verified by theorem provers~\cite{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl}.
15	The standard reward signal in these systems is binary execution feedback: 1 if the solver returns a proof, 0 otherwise.
16	This drives rapid improvement in prove rate but provides no gradient on structural quality---a formalization where every premise contributes to a valid derivation receives the same reward as one that smuggles the conclusion into the premises.
17	Process reward models~\cite{lightman-verify-step} offer finer-grained step-level supervision for mathematical reasoning, but require expensive human annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed verification settings.
18	No existing method provides an annotation-free, solver-agnostic diagnostic for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.
19	
20	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic protocol inspired by mutation testing in software engineering~\cite{demillo1978} that applies component necessity testing---leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs---to quantify structural integrity (\autoref{fig:sta-overview}).
21	For each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR).
22	ISR${=}$1 indicates a maximally tight formalization where every component is individually necessary; ISR${=}$0 indicates complete structural redundancy.
23	The protocol is solver-agnostic, requiring only a solver that accepts component-level input manipulation.
24	Applied as a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\cite{folio} data, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory entirely invisible to prove rate: ISR falls from 0.642 at 100 steps to 0.132 at 500 steps to 0.003 at 1000 steps---a 99.5\% degradation---while prove rate simultaneously rises from 32\% to 96\%.
25	Three random seeds diverge radically (PR $\sigma{=}$47\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse.
26	A supervised fine-tuning (SFT) baseline achieves 92.9\% genuine proofs with near-zero hacking, confirming that the structural collapse is a consequence of RL reward optimization, not model capacity or data limitations.
27	
28	Given ISR's diagnostic power, a natural question is whether it can serve as a training signal to steer models toward structurally sound formalizations.
29	The answer is negative: when ISR is incorporated into the reward ($r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta \cdot \text{ISR})$), the model games the diagnostic metric itself---a phenomenon we characterize as \emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}~\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl}.
30	At low dose ($\beta{=}0.1$), two of three seeds are dominated by \emph{conclusion duplication}: the model generates a single premise identical to the conclusion, achieving ISR${=}$1 by construction while encoding no reasoning.
31	Across three seeds, 64.5\% of proved samples exhibit this ISR-specific gaming mode (H7), compared to 0\% under a matched random-reward control.
32	The remaining seed's apparent ISR advantage is entirely a premise-count artifact: controlling for component count eliminates the difference (1.06$\times$; \S\ref{sec:goodhart}).
33	At high dose ($\beta{=}0.3$), classic Goodhart collapse occurs: prove rate falls to 18\%, compared to 48\% for a matched random control~\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}.
34	This dose-response pattern---low-dose proxy gaming followed by high-dose performance collapse---maps onto established Goodhart dynamics but occurs at the \emph{meta} level: ISR was designed to detect gaming, yet becomes the target of gaming when optimized.
35	
36	We further validate that both the diagnostic utility and the Goodhart phenomenon transfer across formal languages.
37	In Prolog experiments using SWI-Prolog as the solver, exec-only training produces proved-only ISR near zero for two of three seeds, replicating the FOL diagnostic finding.
38	When ISR is added as a Prolog training reward, the result is \emph{universal} Goodhart: all three seeds converge to gaming with 0\% genuine proofs---stronger than the FOL result, where ${\sim}$16\% genuine residual persists---each via a distinct mechanism (tautological collapse, degenerate syntax, and query-as-fact gaming).
39	
40	Across 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 training steps, we make four contributions:
41	
42	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
43	\item We introduce Solver Trace Ablation and establish ISR as a diagnostic that detects reward hacking invisible to prove rate, documenting a complete collapse trajectory ($0.642 \to 0.132 \to 0.003$) over 1000 GRPO training steps; an SFT baseline confirms hacking is RL-specific (92.9\% genuine vs.\ $\leq$19\% mean across RL conditions).
44	\item We empirically characterize meta-level Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic RL---a diagnostic designed to detect gaming becomes gamed when used as reward---and identify the premise-count confound, a systematic LOO bias where fewer components mechanically inflate ISR, that explains the sole apparent improvement (1.06$\times$ premise-matched).
45	\item We demonstrate cross-logic diagnostic transfer from FOL/Prover9 to Prolog/SWI-Prolog, where ISR-as-reward produces universal Goodhart (3/3 seeds gaming, 0\% genuine), demonstrating that simpler formal semantics enable more complete exploitation.
46	\item We catalog 7 reward hacking modes across conditions and logic types, including query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text-match classifiers, motivating functional cross-validation of automated hacking diagnostics.
47	\end{enumerate}
48	
49	\noindent \S\ref{sec:related-work} surveys related work; \S\ref{sec:method} introduces the STA protocol; \S\ref{sec:experiments} presents experiments; \S\ref{sec:discussion} discusses implications.
50	
```

3. 叙事连贯性：Introduction 四贡献 → Experiments 四子节 → Discussion → Conclusion 是否对齐
4. Related Work 定位：与 neurosymbolic RL / reward hacking / mutation testing 三类文献的差异化是否清晰
5. 表格/图表引用：所有 \ref 是否有对应 \label
6. 引用格式：全文应使用 \cite{} 而非 \citep{}/\citet{}

请按维度评分（Originality/Clarity/Soundness/Significance/Presentation, 1-4）+ overall (1-10) + 逐条问题 severity (critical/warning/info)。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01FRLANUh6ArLKE95VEhfrUk`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA-derived ISR across three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}), as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}), and as a cross-logic diagnostic protocol (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We then synthesize the observed hacking strategies into a taxonomy (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
5	
6	\subsection{Setup}
7	\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	\paragraph{Model and training.}
10	We use Qwen3-4B as the base model, trained with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 steps where noted. All experiments use three random seeds (42, 43, 44) to assess cross-seed stability, with seeds controlling model initialization and training stochasticity. Training data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\citep{folio} (\texttt{train\_500\_seed42.jsonl}), a corpus of human-annotated first-order logic problems, held constant across all seeds and conditions.
11	
12	\paragraph{Solvers and logic types.}
13	For first-order logic (FOL), we use Prover9 as the automated theorem prover. For Prolog experiments, we use SWI-Prolog 8.4.2. The execution reward $r_{\text{exec}} \in \{0, 1\}$ is binary: 1 if the solver returns a proof, 0 otherwise.
14	
15	\paragraph{Evaluation.}
16	We evaluate on a held-out set of 100 FOLIO examples using greedy decoding. All ISR values reported in cross-run comparisons are computed by \texttt{eval\_sta.py}, which applies the full STA protocol uniformly. We report: prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, computed over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1), genuine rate, and mean component count where relevant. All tabulated ISR values are proved-only unless otherwise noted.
17	
18	\paragraph{Reward formulations.}
19	We compare six conditions:
20	\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
21	  \item \textbf{Exec-only}: $r = r_{\text{exec}}$ (baseline)
22	  \item \textbf{ISR $\beta$}: $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta \cdot \text{ISR})$, with $\beta \in \{0.1, 0.3\}$
23	  \item \textbf{Random $\beta$}: $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta \cdot u)$, $u \sim \mathcal{U}(0,1)$, matching ISR's range but providing no structural signal
24	  \item \textbf{SFT}: Standard supervised fine-tuning on gold formalizations (no RL)
25	\end{itemize}
26	
27	\paragraph{Output classification.}
28	We classify proved outputs into three categories: \textbf{H7} (single-premise tautology where the sole premise equals the conclusion), \textbf{H1} (multi-premise conclusion embedding where the conclusion appears among the premises), and \textbf{Genuine} (no conclusion duplication detected). Classification uses text matching cross-validated with functional analysis of the LOO necessity distribution ($n_{\text{necessary}}$).
29	
30	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
31	\label{sec:diagnostic}
32	
33	\begin{figure}[t]
34	\centering
35	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}
36	\caption{ISR collapse trajectory under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises from 32\% to 96\% while proved-only ISR falls from 0.642 to 0.003 over 1000 steps. By step~1000, 94.8\% of proved samples have ISR${=}$0. Component count inflates from 6.58 to 18.55, indicating contradiction explosion.}
37	\label{fig:isr-collapse}
38	\end{figure}
39	
40	We first establish that ISR detects structural degeneration invisible to prove rate.
41	Figure~\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).
42	At 100 steps, the model produces structurally sound formalizations: PR${=}$32\%, proved-only ISR${=}$0.642.
43	By 500 steps, PR reaches 93\% but ISR collapses to 0.132---87.1\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1), making proof trivially achievable.
44	At 1000 steps, PR${=}$96\% while ISR drops to 0.003: every proved sample contains the conclusion in its premises (H1${=}$100\%), and the mean component count rises from 6.58 to 18.55.
45	The model generates excessive premises that trivially entail any conclusion via contradiction explosion.
46	The ISR trajectory (0.642 $\to$ 0.132 $\to$ 0.003) is monotonically decreasing with no recovery signal: extended training worsens structural degeneration.
47	
48	\paragraph{Cross-seed instability.}
49	The three exec-only seeds diverge radically, underscoring that single-seed evaluation is unreliable for GRPO-trained formalizers.
50	Seed~42 achieves PR${=}$93\% via conclusion embedding (H1${=}$87.1\%).
51	Seed~43 collapses to PR${=}$14\% with ISR${=}$0.000 through repetition collapse---87\% of outputs hit the maximum token length.
52	Seed~44 reaches PR${=}$98\% but through tautological collapse: the model generates two-component formalizations where the sole premise equals the conclusion (H7${=}$100\%, mean components${=}$2.04).
53	Despite PR values ranging from 14\% to 98\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.
54	The cross-seed PR standard deviation ($\pm$47 percentage points) is itself a finding: GRPO exec-only training is highly unstable, and aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes.
55	
56	\paragraph{SFT baseline.}
57	To confirm that hacking is RL-specific, we train a supervised baseline on gold formalizations.
58	SFT achieves PR${=}$14\% with proved-only ISR${=}$0.571: 92.9\% (13/14) of proved samples are genuine, free of conclusion embedding or duplication.
59	Only 1 of 14 proved samples exhibits H1 (7.1\%), and no H7 instances occur.
60	The near-absence of hacking confirms that the structural degeneration documented above is a consequence of RL reward optimization, not model capacity or data limitations.
61	
62	\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}
63	\label{sec:goodhart}
64	
65	Having established ISR as a diagnostic, we test whether it can serve as a training signal. The answer is negative: ISR-as-reward exhibits \emph{meta-level} Goodhart's Law~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic metric itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.
66	
67	\begin{table*}[t]
68	\centering
69	\small
70	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}
71	\begin{tabular}{llcccccccl}
72	\toprule
73	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Reward} & $\boldsymbol{\beta}$ & $\boldsymbol{n}$ & \textbf{PR\%}$\uparrow$ & \textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \textbf{H7\%} & \textbf{H1\%} & \textbf{Gen.\%} & \textbf{Mode} \\
74	\midrule
75	Base (0-shot) & --- & --- & 1 & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\
76	SFT & supervised & --- & 1 & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \textbf{93} & Genuine \\
77	\midrule
78	Exec-only & $r_e$ & --- & 1$^\dagger$ & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 & H1 embedding \\
79	ISR & $r_e(1{+}\beta{\cdot}\text{ISR})$ & 0.1 & 3 & 77{\scriptsize$\pm$14} & $\ddagger$ & \textbf{65} & 17 & ${\sim}$16 & H7 gaming \\
80	Random & $r_e(1{+}\beta{\cdot}U)$ & 0.1 & 3 & \textbf{85}{\scriptsize$\pm$6} & 0.086{\scriptsize$\pm$.039} & 0 & 81 & 19 & H1 embedding \\
81	\midrule
82	ISR & $r_e(1{+}\beta{\cdot}\text{ISR})$ & 0.3 & 1 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- & PR collapse \\
83	Random & $r_e(1{+}\beta{\cdot}U)$ & 0.3 & 1 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- & Redundant \\
84	\bottomrule
85	\end{tabular}
86	\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. $n$: number of seeds; 3-seed rows show mean$\pm$std. ISR$^*$: proved-only. $U \sim \mathcal{U}(0,1)$; $r_e = r_{\text{exec}}$. $^\dagger$Seed~42 shown; see \S\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\ddagger$ISR omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (64.5\% of proved samples are single-premise tautologies); per-seed ISR in Table~\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}. H7/H1/Gen.\% unavailable for $\beta{=}0.3$ (single seed, not fully classified). Best PR and Gen.\% bolded per group.}
87	\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}
88	\end{table*}
89	
90	\paragraph{Low-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}
91	Table~\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results across conditions. At $\beta{=}0.1$, two of three ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---the model generates a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and high ISR. Across the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\% of proved samples (149/231). In contrast, all three random control seeds show \emph{zero} H7 instances (0/256). H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR reward signal.
92	
93	\begin{table}[t]
94	\centering
95	\small
96	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
97	\begin{tabular}{llccccc}
98	\toprule
99	\textbf{Seed} & \textbf{Cond.} & \textbf{PR\%} & \textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \textbf{H7\%} & \textbf{H1\%} & \textbf{Gen.\%} \\
100	\midrule
101	s42 & ISR & 81 & 0.415 & 16.0 & 51.9 & 30.9 \\
102	s43 & ISR & 61 & 0.830 & \textbf{96.7} & 0 & 3.3 \\
103	s44 & ISR & 89 & 0.902 & \textbf{86.5} & 0 & 13.5$^\dagger$ \\
104	\cmidrule{1-7}
105	 & mean$\pm$std & 77$\pm$14 & --- & 64.5 & 17.3 & ${\sim}$16 \\
106	\midrule
107	s42 & Rand & 79 & 0.131 & 0 & 70.9 & 29.1 \\
108	s43 & Rand & 88 & 0.058 & 0 & \textbf{93.2} & 6.8 \\
109	s44 & Rand & 89 & 0.070 & 0 & 79.8 & 20.2 \\
110	\cmidrule{1-7}
111	 & mean$\pm$std & 85$\pm$6 & 0.086$\pm$.039 & 0 & 81.3$\pm$11 & 18.7$\pm$11 \\
112	\bottomrule
113	\end{tabular}
114	\caption{ISR vs Random at $\beta{=}0.1$: per-seed comparison. ISR$^*$: proved-only. ISR mean omitted for ISR-reward seeds (inflated by H7 gaming). H7 is ISR-specific (64.5\% vs 0\%). H1 is GRPO-universal, higher under random (81.3\%) than ISR (17.3\%)---ISR converts H1 into H7. $^\dagger$s44's 12 ``genuine'' samples all have $n_{\text{prem}}{=}1$ (H7 classifier false negatives). Premise-count-matched genuine ISR: 0.258 (ISR, $n{=}11$) vs 0.243 (random, $n{=}21$) $= 1.06\times$.}
115	\label{tab:beta01-3seed}
116	\end{table}
117	
118	Table~\ref{tab:beta01-3seed} provides the full per-seed breakdown. Conclusion embedding (H1) is a GRPO-universal strategy: it appears at 70.9--93.2\% under random reward and 0--51.9\% under ISR. ISR reward does not reduce total hacking---it \emph{converts} H1 into the more extreme H7 form. Overall conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable (ISR s42: 67.9\% vs Random s42: 70.9\%), and the random control achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\pm$6\% vs 77$\pm$14\%).
119	
120	\paragraph{Premise-count confound.}
121	Seed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw proved-only ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random). However, this apparent advantage is entirely a premise minimization artifact. ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 components vs 8.46 for random), and LOO-based metrics are mathematically biased by component count: fewer premises mean each is more likely to appear necessary, inflating ISR independent of reasoning quality.
122	
123	To control for this, we compare ISR values only among genuine (non-hacking) samples with $\geq$3 premises: ISR genuine achieves 0.258 ($n{=}11$) versus random genuine at 0.243 ($n{=}21$), a ratio of 1.06$\times$---no meaningful structural advantage. Of ISR s42's 26 nominal ``genuine'' samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\geq$0.5 is a mathematical artifact of LOO on short inputs. The raw 2.4$\times$ ISR difference reduces to noise once premise count is controlled.
124	
125	\paragraph{High-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}
126	At $\beta{=}0.3$, ISR reward causes PR to collapse to 18\%---a 5.2$\times$ degradation from exec-only. The matched random control achieves PR${=}$48\%, a 2.7$\times$ advantage, demonstrating that ISR at high dose is not merely unhelpful but \emph{actively harmful} relative to a structurally uninformative signal.
127	
128	\paragraph{Dose-response pattern.}
129	Together, the two dose levels reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}: at low dose ($\beta{=}0.1$), the model games the proxy metric itself (ISR inflation via H7), producing high ISR scores that mask structural degeneration; at high dose ($\beta{=}0.3$), the ISR signal overwhelms the execution reward, causing format collapse and PR degradation. No dose yields genuine structural improvement.
130	
131	\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}
132	\label{sec:crosslogic}
133	
134	To test whether ISR generalizes beyond a single formal language, we replicate the diagnostic and reward experiments using Prolog with SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 as the solver.
135	
136	\begin{figure}[t]
137	\centering
138	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}
139	\caption{Prolog exec-only ISR across three seeds. ISR$^*$ is near-zero for all seeds (s42${=}$0.037, s43${=}$0.015), replicating the FOL finding that exec-only training produces structurally degenerate outputs regardless of PR. Seed~44 ISR$^*{=}$0.387 is a small-sample artifact ($n{=}$21 proved). Cross-seed PR instability ($\sigma{=}$31pp) also replicates.}
140	\label{fig:prolog-comparison}
141	\end{figure}
142	
143	\begin{table}[t]
144	\centering
145	\small
146	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
147	\begin{tabular}{llcccc}
148	\toprule
149	\textbf{Logic} & \textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Seed} & \textbf{PR\%} & \textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \textbf{Mode} \\
150	\midrule
151	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{Exec-only (diagnostic transfer)}} \\
152	FOL & Exec & s42 & 93 & 0.132 & H1 embedding \\
153	FOL & Exec & s43$^\dagger$ & 14 & 0.000 & Repetition \\
154	FOL & Exec & s44 & 98 & 0.582$^\ddagger$ & Tautological \\
155	\cmidrule{1-6}
156	Prolog & Exec & s42 & 83 & 0.037 & Ground facts \\
157	Prolog & Exec & s43 & 56 & 0.015 & Degraded \\
158	Prolog & Exec & s44 & 21 & 0.387$^\S$ & Degraded \\
159	\midrule
160	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\textit{ISR-as-reward (Goodhart transfer)}} \\
161	Prolog & STA & s42 & 50 & 1.000$^\|$ & H7 100\% \\
162	Prolog & STA & s43 & 38 & 0.185$^\|$ & Degen.\ syntax$^\P$ \\
163	Prolog & STA & s44 & 97 & 0.885$^\|$ & Query-as-fact$^\#$ \\
164	\bottomrule
165	\end{tabular}
166	\caption{Cross-logic transfer (proved-only ISR$^*$). \textit{Top}: Exec-only ISR near-zero in both logics, confirming diagnostic transfer. \textit{Bottom}: Prolog STA yields universal Goodhart (3/3 gaming, 0\% genuine). $^\dagger$Sampling eval ($T{=}0.7$); not strictly comparable with greedy-decoded results. $^\ddagger$2-component artifact. $^\S$Small-sample ($n{=}$21). $^\|$Gaming-inflated. $^\P$Uppercase pseudo-Prolog; $n_{\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38. $^\#$NL$\to$snake\_case atom as fact+query; $n_{\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97.}
167	\label{tab:prolog-crosslogic}
168	\end{table}
169	
170	\paragraph{Diagnostic transfer.}
171	Table~\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} (top) and Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison} show that ISR detects structural degeneration in Prolog as it does in FOL. Two of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}$0.037, s43${=}$0.015), confirming structurally degenerate outputs despite moderate-to-high PR. Seed~44 (ISR$^*{=}$0.387) is a small-sample artifact: only 21 of 100 samples prove, and the higher ISR reflects variance from this sparse denominator. Cross-seed PR instability in FOL ($\sigma{=}$47pp) replicates in Prolog ($\sigma{=}$31pp), and both logic types produce qualitatively different collapse modes across seeds.
172	
173	We note an important semantic distinction: low ISR in Prolog exec-only reflects \emph{redundant coding}---the model generates repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof---rather than absence of reasoning. This contrasts with the high ISR under Prolog STA (below), which reflects \emph{metric gaming}. ISR must be interpreted relative to the training objective.
174	
175	\paragraph{Universal Goodhart in Prolog.}
176	Table~\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} (bottom) reveals the strongest Goodhart result: all three Prolog STA seeds converge to gaming, with 0\% genuine proofs across all 185 proved samples. Each seed develops a distinct gaming pathway:
177	\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
178	  \item \textbf{s42}: Clean H7---a single Prolog clause identical to the query (50/50 proved, ISR$^*{=}$1.000 because the sole component is trivially necessary).
179	  \item \textbf{s43}: Degenerate syntax---uppercase pseudo-Prolog (e.g., \texttt{MARY\_IS\_AN\_AVADEOTE.}). A text-match classifier labels these as genuine, but functional analysis shows $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$ for all 38 proved samples (functionally H7).
180	  \item \textbf{s44}: Query-as-fact gaming---the model converts NL problems to snake\_case atoms and sets them as both fact and query, achieving PR${=}$97\% with $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$ for 95/97. Initial automated classification reported 100\% genuine; independent functional analysis corrected this to 0\%.
181	\end{itemize}
182	This result is stronger than the FOL finding: FOL ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ retains ${\sim}$16\% genuine residual across seeds, while Prolog STA produces zero. Prolog's simpler semantics enable more complete exploitation, eliminating the genuine residual observed in FOL.
183	
184	\paragraph{Classifier limitations.}
185	The Prolog results expose a blind spot of text-match hacking classifiers. Seeds~s43 and s44 bypass the strict H7/H1 classifier through surface-level novelty (non-standard casing, atom-level encoding) while remaining functionally tautological ($n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$). This motivates cross-validation of hacking classifiers with functional metrics such as the $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution.
186	
187	\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}
188	\label{sec:taxonomy}
189	
190	\begin{figure}[t]
191	\centering
192	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}
193	\caption{Hacking mode composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR reward converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into the more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking prevalence. SFT exhibits near-zero hacking (92.9\% genuine).}
194	\label{fig:hacking-composition}
195	\end{figure}
196	
197	\begin{table}[t]
198	\centering
199	\small
200	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
201	\begin{tabular}{p{1.6cm}p{2.0cm}cccc}
202	\toprule
203	\textbf{Mode} & \textbf{Mechanism} & \textbf{Exec} & \textbf{ISR} & \textbf{Rand} & \textbf{SFT} \\
204	\midrule
205	H1 & Concl.\ among premises & 87 & 17 & 81 & 7 \\
206	H7 & Single prem.\ = concl. & $\bullet$ & \textbf{65} & 0 & 0 \\
207	Tautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\
208	Repetition & Output degen. & $\bullet$ & --- & --- & --- \\
209	Contradiction & $n_{\text{comp}}{\uparrow}$, ISR${\to}$0 & $\bullet$ & --- & --- & --- \\
210	C-w/o-util. & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\
211	Q-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & --- & --- & --- & --- \\
212	\bottomrule
213	\end{tabular}
214	\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed means. Exec: H1${=}$87\% in s42; $\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes (H7/Tautological in s44, Repetition in s43, Contradiction at 1000~steps). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).}
215	\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}
216	\end{table}
217	
218	Across all conditions, seeds, and logic types, we identify seven distinct hacking modes (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}, Figure~\ref{fig:hacking-composition}):
219	
220	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
221	  \item \textbf{H1: Conclusion embedding.} The conclusion appears among multiple premises; the solver succeeds trivially. GRPO-universal: 52--93\% of proved samples across all RL reward types.
222	  \item \textbf{H7: Conclusion duplication.} A single premise identical to the conclusion. ISR-specific: 64.5\% under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ vs 0\% under random.
223	  \item \textbf{Tautological collapse.} Two-component formalizations where premise${=}$conclusion (exec-only s44, 94\% of outputs, mean components${=}$2.04).
224	  \item \textbf{Repetition collapse.} Identical premises repeated until the token limit (exec-only s43, 87\% hit max length).
225	  \item \textbf{Contradiction explosion.} Excessive premises (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps) that trivially entail any conclusion via embedded contradictions.
226	  \item \textbf{Complexity-without-utility.} Syntactically complex but semantically vacuous premises (random $\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\%).
227	  \item \textbf{Query-as-fact gaming.} NL problems converted to snake\_case atoms serving as both fact and query (Prolog STA s44). Bypasses text-match classifiers while remaining functionally tautological.
228	\end{enumerate}
229	
230	The reward signal determines hacking \emph{composition} but not \emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random conditions (${\sim}$68--81\%), but ISR converts the GRPO-universal H1 mode into the ISR-specific H7 mode. SFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (92.9\% genuine), confirming that hacking is RL-reward-specific. Different reward formulations produce characteristic hacking signatures---a finding that may inform future diagnostic design.
231	
```

consequence of RL reward optimization, not model capacity or data limitations.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nHaving established ISR as a diagnostic, we test whether it can serve as a training signal. The answer is negative: ISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart's Law~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic metric itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.5pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcccccccl}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Reward} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & $\\boldsymbol{n}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} & \\textbf{Mode} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & --- & 1 & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & supervised & --- & 1 & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} & Genuine \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only & $r_e$ & --- & 1$^\\dagger$ & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 & H1 embedding \\\\\nISR & $r_e(1{+}\\beta{\\cdot}\\text{ISR})$ & 0.1 & 3 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 & H7 gaming \\\\\nRandom & $r_e(1{+}\\beta{\\cdot}U)$ & 0.1 & 3 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.039} & 0 & 81 & 19 & H1 embedding \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & $r_e(1{+}\\beta{\\cdot}\\text{ISR})$ & 0.3 & 1 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- & PR collapse \\\\\nRandom & $r_e(1{+}\\beta{\\cdot}U)$ & 0.3 & 1 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- & Redundant \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. $n$: number of seeds; 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std. ISR$^*$: proved-only. $U \\sim \\mathcal{U}(0,1)$; $r_e = r_{\\text{exec}}$. $^\\dagger$Seed~42 shown; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (64.5\\% of proved samples are single-premise tautologies); per-seed ISR in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}. H7/H1/Gen.\\% unavailable for $\\beta{=}0.3$ (single seed, not fully classified). Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\paragraph{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results across conditions. At $\\beta{=}0.1$, two of three ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---the model generates a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and high ISR. Across the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231). In contrast, all three random control seeds show \\emph{zero} H7 instances (0/256). H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR reward signal.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Cond.} & \\textbf{PR\\%} & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\ns42 & ISR & 81 & 0.415 & 16.0 & 51.9 & 30.9 \\\\\ns43 & ISR & 61 & 0.830 & \\textbf{96.7} & 0 & 3.3 \\\\\ns44 & ISR & 89 & 0.902 & \\textbf{86.5} & 0 & 13.5$^\\dagger$ \\\\\n\\cmidrule{1-7}\n & mean$\\pm$std & 77$\\pm$14 & --- & 64.5 & 17.3 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\n\\midrule\ns42 & Rand & 79 & 0.131 & 0 & 70.9 & 29.1 \\\\\ns43 & Rand & 88 & 0.058 & 0 & \\textbf{93.2} & 6.8 \\\\\ns44 & Rand & 89 & 0.070 & 0 & 79.8 & 20.2 \\\\\n\\cmidrule{1-7}\n & mean$\\pm$std & 85$\\pm$6 & 0.086$\\pm$.039 & 0 & 81.3$\\pm$11 & 18.7$\\pm$11 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{ISR vs Random at $\\beta{=}0.1$: per-seed comparison. ISR$^*$: proved-only. ISR mean omitted for ISR-reward seeds (inflated by H7 gaming). H7 is ISR-specific (64.5\\% vs 0\\%). H1 is GRPO-universal, higher under random (81.3\\%) than ISR (17.3\\%)---ISR converts H1 into H7. $^\\dagger$s44's 12 ``genuine'' samples all have $n_{\\text{prem}}{=}1$ (H7 classifier false negatives). Premise-count-matched genuine ISR: 0.258 (ISR, $n{=}11$) vs 0.243 (random, $n{=}21$) $= 1.06\\times$.}\n\\label{tab:beta01-3seed}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed} provides the full per-seed breakdown. Conclusion embedding (H1) is a GRPO-universal strategy: it appears at 70.9--93.2\\% under random reward and 0--51.9\\% under ISR. ISR reward does not reduce total hacking---it \\emph{converts} H1 into the more extreme H7 form. Overall conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable (ISR s42: 67.9\\% vs Random s42: 70.9\\%), and the random control achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\paragraph{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw proved-only ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random). However, this apparent advantage is entirely a premise minimization artifact. ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 components vs 8.46 for random), and LOO-based metrics are mathematically biased by component count: fewer premises mean each is more likely to appear necessary, inflating ISR independent of reasoning quality.\n\nTo control for this, we compare ISR values only among genuine (non-hacking) samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR genuine achieves 0.258 ($n{=}11$) versus random genuine at 0.243 ($n{=}21$), a ratio of 1.06$\\times$---no meaningful structural advantage. Of ISR s42's 26 nominal ``genuine'' samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq$0.5 is a mathematical artifact of LOO on short inputs. The raw 2.4$\\times$ ISR difference reduces to noise once premise count is controlled.\n\n\\paragraph{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR reward causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only. The matched random control achieves PR${=}$48\\%, a 2.7$\\times$ advantage, demonstrating that ISR at high dose is not merely unhelpful but \\emph{actively harmful} relative to a structurally uninformative signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Dose-response pattern.}\nTogether, the two dose levels reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}: at low dose ($\\beta{=}0.1$), the model games the proxy metric itself (ISR inflation via H7), producing high ISR scores that mask structural degeneration; at high dose ($\\beta{=}0.3$), the ISR signal overwhelms the execution reward, causing format collapse and PR degradation. No dose yields genuine structural improvement.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nTo test whether ISR generalizes beyond a single formal language, we replicate the diagnostic and reward experiments using Prolog with SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 as the solver.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Prolog exec-only ISR across three seeds. ISR$^*$ is near-zero for all seeds (s42${=}$0.037, s43${=}$0.015), replicating the FOL finding that exec-only training produces structurally degenerate outputs regardless of PR. Seed~44 ISR$^*{=}$0.387 is a small-sample artifact ($n{=}$21 proved). Cross-seed PR instability ($\\sigma{=}$31pp) also replicates.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Logic} & \\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR\\%} & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{Mode} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Exec-only (diagnostic transfer)}} \\\\\nFOL & Exec & s42 & 93 & 0.132 & H1 embedding \\\\\nFOL & Exec & s43$^\\dagger$ & 14 & 0.000 & Repetition \\\\\nFOL & Exec & s44 & 98 & 0.582$^\\ddagger$ & Tautological \\\\\n\\cmidrule{1-6}\nProlog & Exec & s42 & 83 & 0.037 & Ground facts \\\\\nProlog & Exec & s43 & 56 & 0.015 & Degraded \\\\\nProlog & Exec & s44 & 21 & 0.387$^\\S$ & Degraded \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{ISR-as-reward (Goodhart transfer)}} \\\\\nProlog & STA & s42 & 50 & 1.000$^\\|$ & H7 100\\% \\\\\nProlog & STA & s43 & 38 & 0.185$^\\|$ & Degen.\\ syntax$^\\P$ \\\\\nProlog & STA & s44 & 97 & 0.885$^\\|$ & Query-as-fact$^\\#$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Cross-logic transfer (proved-only ISR$^*$). \\textit{Top}: Exec-only ISR near-zero in both logics, confirming diagnostic transfer. \\textit{Bottom}: Prolog STA yields universal Goodhart (3/3 gaming, 0\\% genuine). $^\\dagger$Sampling eval ($T{=}0.7$); not strictly comparable with greedy-decoded results. $^\\ddagger$2-component artifact. $^\\S$Small-sample ($n{=}$21). $^\\|$Gaming-inflated. $^\\P$Uppercase pseudo-Prolog; $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 38/38. $^\\#$NL$\\to$snake\\_case atom as fact+query; $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$ for 95/97.}\n\\label{tab:prolog-crosslogic}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic transfer.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} (top) and Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison} show that ISR detects structural degeneration in Prolog as it does in FOL. Two of three Prolog exec-only seeds yield proved-only ISR near zero (s42${=}$0.037, s43${=}$0.015), confirming structurally degenerate outputs despite moderate-to-high PR. Seed~44 (ISR$^*{=}$0.387) is a small-sample artifact: only 21 of 100 samples prove, and the higher ISR reflects variance from this sparse denominator. Cross-seed PR instability in FOL ($\\sigma{=}$47pp) replicates in Prolog ($\\sigma{=}$31pp), and both logic types produce qualitatively different collapse modes across seeds.\n\nWe note an important semantic distinction: low ISR in Prolog exec-only reflects \\emph{redundant coding}---the model generates repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof---rather than absence of reasoning. This contrasts with the high ISR under Prolog STA (below), which reflects \\emph{metric gaming}. ISR must be interpreted relative to the training objective.\n\n\\paragraph{Universal Goodhart in Prolog.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} (bottom) reveals the strongest Goodhart result: all three Prolog STA seeds converge to gaming, with 0\\% genuine proofs across all 185 proved samples. Each seed develops a distinct gaming pathway:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n  \\item \\textbf{s42}: Clean H7---a single Prolog clause identical to the query (50/50 proved, ISR$^*{=}$1.000 because the sole component is trivially necessary).\n  \\item \\textbf{s43}: Degenerate syntax---uppercase pseudo-Prolog (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVADEOTE.}). A text-match classifier labels these as genuine, but functional analysis shows $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ for all 38 proved samples (functionally H7).\n  \\item \\textbf{s44}: Query-as-fact gaming---the model converts NL problems to snake\\_case atoms and sets them as both fact and query, achieving PR${=}$97\\% with $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ for 95/97. Initial automated classification reported 100\\% genuine; independent functional analysis corrected this to 0\\%.\n\\end{itemize}\nThis result is stronger than the FOL finding: FOL ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ retains ${\\sim}$16\\% genuine residual across seeds, while Prolog STA produces zero. Prolog's simpler semantics enable more complete exploitation, eliminating the genuine residual observed in FOL.\n\n\\paragraph{Classifier limitations.}\nThe Prolog results expose a blind spot of text-match hacking classifiers. Seeds~s43 and s44 bypass the strict H7/H1 classifier through surface-level novelty (non-standard casing, atom-level encoding) while remaining functionally tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$). This motivates cross-validation of hacking classifiers with functional metrics such as the $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking mode composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR reward converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into the more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking prevalence. SFT exhibits near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{p{1.6cm}p{2.0cm}cccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rand} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ among premises & 87 & 17 & 81 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{65} & 0 & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}{\\uparrow}$, ISR${\\to}$0 & $\\bullet$ & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nC-w/o-util. & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed means. Exec: H1${=}$87\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes (H7/Tautological in s44, Repetition in s43, Contradiction at 1000~steps). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}\n\\end{table}\n\nAcross all conditions, seeds, and logic types, we identify seven distinct hacking modes (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}, Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}):\n\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n  \\item \\textbf{H1: Conclusion embedding.} The conclusion appears among multiple premises; the solver succeeds trivially. GRPO-universal: 52--93\\% of proved samples across all RL reward types.\n  \\item \\textbf{H7: Conclusion duplication.} A single premise identical to the conclusion. ISR-specific: 64.5\\% under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs 0\\% under random.\n  \\item \\textbf{Tautological collapse.} Two-component formalizations where premise${=}$conclusion (exec-only s44, 94\\% of outputs, mean components${=}$2.04).\n  \\item \\textbf{Repetition collapse.} Identical premises repeated until the token limit (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length).\n  \\item \\textbf{Contradiction explosion.} Excessive premises (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps) that trivially entail any conclusion via embedded contradictions.\n  \\item \\textbf{Complexity-without-utility.} Syntactically complex but semantically vacuous premises (random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%).\n  \\item \\textbf{Query-as-fact gaming.} NL problems converted to snake\\_case atoms serving as both fact and query (Prolog STA s44). Bypasses text-match classifiers while remaining functionally tautological.\n\\end{enumerate}\n\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random conditions (${\\sim}$68--81\\%), but ISR converts the GRPO-universal H1 mode into the ISR-specific H7 mode. SFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine), confirming that hacking is RL-reward-specific. Different reward formulations produce characteristic hacking signatures---a finding that may inform future diagnostic design.\n",

1	\begin{abstract}
2	GRPO-trained neurosymbolic formalizers achieve high prove rates---93\% on FOLIO---yet 87\% of proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises, a degenerate shortcut invisible to execution-based evaluation. We introduce \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a solver-agnostic diagnostic that applies leave-one-out component necessity testing to solver inputs. The fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR): ISR$=$1 indicates maximal tightness; ISR$=$0 indicates complete redundancy.
3	
4	ISR exposes a collapse trajectory hidden from prove rate: over 100 to 1000 GRPO training steps (Qwen3-4B), ISR falls from 0.642 to 0.003---a 99.5\% degradation---while prove rate rises from 32\% to 96\%. An SFT baseline achieves 92.9\% genuine proofs with near-zero hacking, confirming the degeneration is RL-specific.
5	
6	Can ISR serve as a training signal? Incorporating ISR into the reward at $\beta{=}0.1$ induces \emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}: 64.5\% of proved samples exhibit conclusion duplication (H7), versus 0\% under matched random controls. The sole apparent ISR improvement reduces to $1.06\times$ after controlling for a premise-count confound. At $\beta{=}0.3$, prove rate collapses to 18\% versus 48\% for random control.
7	
8	Cross-logic transfer to Prolog/SWI-Prolog yields a stronger result: ISR-as-reward produces universal Goodhart---3/3 seeds gaming via distinct mechanisms, 0\% genuine proofs.
9	
10	Across 6 reward conditions, 2 logic types (FOL/Prover9, Prolog/SWI-Prolog), and 3 seeds, we catalog 7 distinct reward hacking modes---including query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text-match classifiers---documenting ISR's fundamental asymmetry: reliable as a diagnostic, unreliable as an optimization target.
11	\end{abstract}
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic Reasoning with LLMs.}
5	Prompting-based pipelines couple LLMs with external solvers to offload logical inference.
6	Logic-LM~\cite{logic-lm} generates FOL formalizations verified by an external prover, with a self-refinement loop on solver errors; LINC~\cite{linc} similarly translates natural language to first-order logic for Prover9, demonstrating gains over chain-of-thought on syllogistic and multi-hop tasks.
7	SatLM~\cite{satlm} extends the paradigm to satisfiability solvers via declarative prompting, while LoRP~\cite{lorp} and FoVer~\cite{fover-pei2025} route through Prolog execution traces and FOL verification, respectively, to validate or filter LLM-generated reasoning.
8	All of these systems evaluate on execution accuracy---whether the solver returns the correct answer---without diagnosing the structural quality of the generated formalizations.
9	RL-based training represents a more recent direction.
10	Mellgren~\cite{mellgren-prolog-grpo} applies GRPO to train Prolog formalizers with binary solver reward, reporting substantial prove-rate gains; Logic-RL~\cite{logic-rl} demonstrates that rule-based RL with solver feedback elicits emergent reasoning in LLMs; and Thought-Like-Pro~\cite{thought-like-pro} uses self-driven Prolog chain-of-thought to enhance multi-step reasoning.
11	These RL systems optimize execution reward exclusively, reporting prove rate as the primary success metric.
12	Our work differs in diagnostic focus: STA reveals that execution accuracy conflates faithful derivation with degenerate shortcuts, and ISR quantifies this conflation.
13	The finding that 87\% of proved outputs under exec-only GRPO embed the conclusion among the premises (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}) applies directly to these systems, suggesting that reported prove-rate gains may overstate genuine reasoning improvement.
14	
15	\paragraph{Reward Shaping and Process Supervision.}
16	Binary outcome rewards provide no gradient on intermediate reasoning quality, motivating process-level supervision.
17	Lightman et al.~\cite{lightman-verify-step} train process reward models (PRMs) that score individual reasoning steps, improving mathematical problem-solving over outcome-only rewards.
18	Math-Shepherd~\cite{math-shepherd} automates step-level annotation by validating intermediate states against reference solutions.
19	Beyond learned PRMs, symbolic feedback offers an alternative grounding: RLSF~\cite{rlsf} uses solver diagnostics as a structured training signal for formal reasoning, and Rubric-GRPO~\cite{rubric-grpo} replaces scalar rewards with multi-dimensional rubric scores evaluated by an LLM judge.
20	However, process rewards introduce their own failure modes.
21	Gao et al.~\cite{gao-effective-rl-reward} show that reward design choices---granularity, normalization, aggregation---can dominate the effect of the underlying signal.
22	PROF~\cite{prof-process-outcome} finds that process and outcome rewards conflict under joint optimization, requiring explicit harmonization.
23	The Qwen PRM study~\cite{qwen-prm-lessons} documents that PRMs degrade unpredictably at distribution shift, and ProGRS~\cite{progrs-outcome-conditioned} introduces outcome-conditioned process rewards to mitigate the gap between step-level correctness and final-answer accuracy.
24	ISR differs from these approaches in that it is solver-grounded rather than learned: it derives directly from deterministic LOO ablation of solver inputs, eliminating reward model noise.
25	Our finding that ISR reliably \emph{diagnoses} structural quality yet becomes the target of gaming when used as a training reward (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}) adds an empirical data point to this literature: even non-learned process signals can Goodhart when optimized.
26	
27	\paragraph{Reward Hacking and Goodhart's Law.}
28	Gao et al.~\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization} establish scaling laws for reward model overoptimization, showing that proxy reward increases monotonically with optimization pressure until true performance collapses.
29	Manheim and Garrabrant~\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} taxonomize four variants of Goodhart's Law---regressional, extremal, causal, and adversarial---while Skalse et al.~\cite{skalse-reward-hacking} prove that reward hacking is generically unavoidable for imperfect proxies.
30	Karwowski et al.~\cite{karwowski-goodhart-rl} demonstrate Goodhart dynamics geometrically in RL, showing that KL regularization delays but does not prevent overoptimization; Pan et al.~\cite{pan-reward-misspecification} map how misspecified rewards produce qualitatively different failure modes depending on the degree of misalignment; and Kwa et al.~\cite{kwa-catastrophic-goodhart} show that KL divergence regularization fails catastrophically under heavy-tailed reward misspecification.
31	In the LLM setting specifically, Bondarenko et al.~\cite{bondarenko-spec-gaming} demonstrate specification gaming in reasoning models, Kim et al.~\cite{kim-game-formalization} evaluate whether LLMs game logical formalization tasks, and Yang et al.~\cite{spurious-rewards} identify spurious reward signals in RLVR that inflate metrics without genuine capability gains.
32	This literature analyzes Goodhart dynamics either theoretically or within RLHF/text generation, where the proxy is a learned reward model.
33	Our setting differs in two respects: the proxy (ISR) is deterministic and solver-derived, eliminating reward model noise as an explanation; and the Goodhart effect operates at a \emph{meta} level---ISR was designed to \emph{detect} gaming of execution reward, yet itself becomes gamed when optimized.
34	The dose-response pattern we observe ($\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming via conclusion duplication; $\beta{=}0.3$: performance collapse) traces the trajectory predicted by Gao et al.~\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization} within a single neurosymbolic RL setup, and the premise-count confound that explains away apparent ISR improvements provides a concrete mechanism for regressional Goodhart in formal reasoning.
35	
36	\paragraph{Mutation Testing and Component Analysis.}
37	Mutation testing originated with DeMillo et al.~\cite{demillo1978}, who proposed injecting systematic faults into programs to assess test suite adequacy; the coupling effect hypothesis~\cite{offutt-coupling} provides the theoretical grounding that simple mutants subsume complex faults.
38	Jia and Harman~\cite{jia-harman-survey} survey three decades of development, establishing leave-one-out and higher-order mutation as the dominant paradigm, while Just et al.~\cite{just-mutants-real-faults} validate that synthetic mutants approximate real faults in detection experiments.
39	Recent work connects mutation testing with LLMs: LLMorpheus~\cite{llmorpheus} uses language models to generate semantically rich mutants that go beyond syntactic operators.
40	STA adapts the core LOO intuition to a different domain---formal logic formalizations---where the ``program under test'' is the solver input and the ``test'' is proof success.
41	The unit of mutation shifts from code statements to logical components (premises in FOL, clauses in Prolog), and the diagnostic target shifts from test adequacy to structural necessity for proof validity.
42	The novelty lies not in the perturbation technique itself but in its application to neurosymbolic RL and the empirical findings it enables: the ISR collapse trajectory, the Goodhart phenomenon, and the taxonomy of hacking modes.
43	
44	\paragraph{Positioning.}
45	This work sits at the intersection of neurosymbolic reasoning, reward design, and Goodhart dynamics.
46	Where prior neurosymbolic systems evaluate on execution accuracy alone, STA provides a structural diagnostic; where prior reward-shaping work assumes that finer-grained signals safely improve training, our ISR-as-reward experiments demonstrate meta-level Goodhart failure; and where prior Goodhart analyses study learned reward models, we show the same dynamics arise from a deterministic, solver-grounded metric.
47	The mutation-testing connection provides the methodological foundation, but the contributions are empirical: structural collapse under RL, meta-level gaming of the diagnostic itself, and a taxonomy of exploitation strategies across formal languages.
48	
```
